# Initialization

## 1. OVERVIEW

Sets up the deep research packet, canonical state files, and lineage mode before the loop begins.

Initialization is the workflow-owned entry into a research session. It creates the packet directory, seeds the state surfaces that the LEAF agent will read later, and decides whether the run is a fresh start, a resume, or a restart.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The live initialization contract classifies the packet state before it writes anything. It distinguishes fresh state, resumable state, completed-session state, and invalid state. Fresh runs create `research/deep-research-config.json`, the first JSONL line in `research/deep-research-state.jsonl`, `research/deep-research-strategy.md`, and `research/findings-registry.json`. Confirm mode adds a charter review gate before the first iteration continues.

Lineage handling is narrower than some older drafts. The runtime supports `new`, `resume`, `restart`, and `completed-continue`. Resume keeps the same session identifier and appends a typed `resumed` event. Restart archives the old research tree, mints a fresh session identifier, increments generation, and appends a typed `restarted` event. `completed-continue` is live through `--extend=N` on a completed run: it keeps the session identifier and generation, raises `maxIterations` by N, appends a typed `run_extended` event, and copies the prior synthesis to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration.

`fork` remains deferred and is not exposed as a live mode.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command | Defines the init phase inputs, packet outputs, and setup contract before the YAML workflow loads. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | Creates canonical state files on the initialization path. Confirm mode adds charter review and state checks. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` | Reference | Documents session classification, canonical names, and the resumed or restarted lifecycle event contract. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/deep-research-config.json` | Asset | Supplies the default config shape written during initialization. |

title: "Convergence check"
description: "Chooses whether the loop continues, enters stuck recovery, or reaches a legal stop."
trigger_phrases:

  - "check convergence"
  - "legal-stop gates"
  - "blocked-stop handling"
  - "statistical stop vote"
version: 1.14.1.0
---

# Convergence check

## 1. OVERVIEW

Applies the statistical stop vote, legal-stop gates, and blocked-stop handling before research can end.

Convergence checking is the control point between one finished iteration and the next decision. It decides whether the loop continues, enters recovery mode, or can move into synthesis.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The live loop applies convergence in layers. Hard stops for `maxIterations` and all-questions-answered run first. convergence.cjs evaluates the weighted `shouldContinue()` signals from `convergence.md`, and when those signals or full question coverage nominate STOP, it applies the legal-stop bundle rather than stopping on novelty math alone.

That legal-stop bundle is workflow-visible, not only prose guidance. The workflow runs convergence.cjs with `--persist-snapshot` and `--stop-decision`, records the graph convergence result, and persists a first-class `blocked_stop` event when the decision is blocked. That event carries gate results, blockers, recovery guidance, session identifier, and generation so the reducer and dashboard can show why the loop had to continue.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md` | Reference | Defines the hard stops, weighted signals, legal-stop bundle, and stop labels. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` | Reference | Defines the loop ordering, guard step, and blocked-stop persistence contract. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | Runs convergence.cjs for the stop decision and persists `blocked_stop` events. Confirm mode adds approval gates. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/composite-convergence-stop-behavior.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies the composite convergence stop behavior. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/convergence-passes-guard-fails-override.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies that a nominated stop is overridden when the legal-stop guards fail. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/blocked-stop-reducer-surfacing.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies blocked-stop events surface through reducer-owned packet outputs. |

# DR-072 -- Frontier open veto holds a soft stop

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-072`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `frontierOpen` veto in the stop decision. The objective is to confirm that a soft stop waits while frontier nodes stay open, and that every hard rule keeps its precedence.

### Why This Matters

A research run that answers its init questions narrowly looks converged. The veto keeps the run going while the frontier still holds open work. It must never outrank a hard limit, or a run could loop past its budget.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the veto precedence table and the unchanged input echo.

- Objective: Confirm that an open node vetoes a composite convergence stop only under the expansive mode, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy keep their own decisions.
- Real user request: Explain why the run did not stop when it looked converged, and show that the hard limits still stop it.
- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a composite convergence stop continues while frontier nodes are open, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the veto table by file stem, run the input echo case, then read the veto branch and the research facts in the stop-decision script.
- Expected signals: The veto table passes all eight rows. The row for open nodes returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`. The row for an empty frontier returns `STOP` with `composite_converged`. The cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and max-iterations rows keep their own decisions. The echo case passes unedited.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a soft stop waits for open frontier nodes, and the hard limits still stop the run on time.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the table and the echo case pass and the script shows the veto after the floor; FAIL if an open node lets a stop through, or a hard limit loses to the veto.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a composite convergence stop continues while frontier nodes are open, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision.vitest -t "frontierOpen veto" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision.vitest -t "echoes the input unchanged" --reporter=verbose`
3. `rg -n "frontierOpen" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  15 passed | 64 skipped (79)`. Those cases include the eight rows of the veto table, each named `expansive composite candidate: <row label>`, and every row passes. Command 2 prints `Tests  1 passed | 78 skipped (79)`, and the case `echoes the input unchanged` passes without edits. Command 3 prints the veto branch that returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`, and the research facts line that counts open and active nodes.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary line from commands 1 and 2, with each exit status.
- The `rg` lines from command 3, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 and 2 exit 0 with the expected counts, and command 3 shows the veto after the floor check.
- **Fail**: An open node lets a stop through, a hard limit loses to the veto, or the echo case fails.

### Failure Triage

- An open node lets a stop through: check that the veto reads the expansive flag and the `frontierOpen` count from the research facts.
- A hard limit loses to the veto: check the order of the veto and the cap, floor and stuck checks in the decision.
- The echo case fails: check that the stop-decision config echo adds `depthMode` only for an expansive run.

title: "DR-070 -- Frontier admission prunes each proposal with its reason"

# DR-070 -- Frontier admission prunes each proposal with its reason

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-070`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the admission step that turns a leaf's `frontier_proposal` rows into questions. The objective is to confirm that a proposal failing a check is pruned with the reason of the first failing check, and that only an admitted proposal writes an inbox row.

### Why This Matters

Leaves over-propose. Admission is the gate that keeps the question set bounded, so each rejection must name its cause. An operator who reads a pruned row must be able to tell which bound it hit.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the six reasons, the prune cases and the admit case.

- Objective: Confirm that each failing check prunes with its own reason, that the first failing check wins and that a pruned proposal never reaches the inbox.
- Real user request: Show me why a proposed sub-question was rejected, and show me that a valid one was added.
- Prompt: `Check that every frontier proposal that fails an admission check is pruned with that check's reason, and that only the admitted proposal reaches the research inbox with origin frontier. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Read the reason returns in the admission helper, then run the prune rows, the admit case and the pruned-record case by file stem.
- Expected signals: The helper returns the six reasons, `relevance`, `dedupe`, `depth`, `fanout`, `budget` and `cycle`. The seven prune rows pass. The admitted proposal writes exactly one inbox row with `origin: "frontier"`. The pruned proposal writes a store row and no inbox row.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can name the bound each rejected proposal hit, and can see the one proposal that entered the question set.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the six reasons appear, all seven prune rows and both record cases pass; FAIL if a proposal is pruned with another check's reason, or a pruned proposal reaches the inbox.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that every frontier proposal that fails an admission check is pruned with that check's reason, and that only the admitted proposal reaches the research inbox with origin frontier. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n "return '(relevance|dedupe|depth|fanout|budget|cycle)'" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "prunes with" --reporter=verbose`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "admits a valid proposal" --reporter=verbose`
4. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "records a pruned proposal" --reporter=verbose`

### Expected

Command 1 prints 13 lines. Every one of the six reason strings appears, because some reasons are returned from more than one guard. Command 2 prints `Tests  7 passed | 17 skipped (24)`, with seven prune rows: two for `relevance`, and one each for `dedupe`, `depth`, `fanout`, `budget` and `cycle`. Command 3 prints `Tests  1 passed | 23 skipped (24)`, and that case asserts one inbox row with `origin: "frontier"` and one open store row. Command 4 prints `Tests  1 passed | 23 skipped (24)`, and that case asserts a store row with no inbox line.

### Evidence

- The `rg` lines from command 1 and the verbose case lines from commands 2 to 4.
- The summary line and exit status for each command.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: All six reasons appear, every command exits 0 with the expected pass count and no failed count.
- **Fail**: A proposal is pruned with another check's reason, a pruned proposal reaches the inbox, or any command fails.

### Failure Triage

- A proposal is pruned with the wrong reason: check the order of the checks in the admission helper, since the first failing check must win.
- A pruned proposal reaches the inbox: check that the inbox write follows only an admitted result.
- A reason string is missing from command 1: a check no longer returns its reason, so restore the return before trusting the verdict.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '320,380p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs
// 2. dedupe
  const key = dedupeKey(proposal.question);
  if (key === '') return 'dedupe';
  for (const node of nodes.values()) {
    // Any status counts: re-proposing a closed branch would reopen work the run
    // already settled, whether it was answered, exhausted or pruned.
    if (nodeDedupeKey(node) === key) return 'dedupe';
  }
  if (Array.isArray(knownTexts)) {
    for (const text of knownTexts) {
      if (typeof text === 'string' && dedupeKey(text) === key) return 'dedupe';
    }
  }

  // 3. depth
  const depth = depthFor(proposal, nodes);
  if (depth > caps.maxDepth) return 'depth';

  // 4. fan-out
  if (countLiveChildren(nodes, proposal.parentId) >= childCapFor(proposal.parentId, caps)) return 'fanout';
  if (countLiveNodes(nodes) >= caps.maxFrontierNodes) return 'fanout';

  // 5. budget
  const parent = proposal.parentId === 'root' ? null : nodes.get(proposal.parentId);
  if (Number.isFinite(proposal.budget) && parent && Number.isFinite(parent.budget)) {
    // Budgets only narrow from parent to child; a child that claims more than
    // its parent could spend work the run never allotted to that branch.
    if (proposal.budget > parent.budget) return 'budget';
  }

  // 6. cycle
  return checkCycle(proposal.parentId, nodes, caps);
}

function makeProposal(question: string): NodeRow {
  return { parentId: 'root', question, scope: 'runtime/lib', evidence: 'runtime/lib/a.ts:10' };
}

function nodeRow(
  nodeId: string,
  parentId: string | null,
  depth: number,
  question: string,
  extra: NodeRow = {},
): NodeRow {
  return { nodeId, parentId, depth, question, status: 'open', reason: null, iteration: 0, ...extra };
}

function readJsonl(filePath: string): NodeRow[] {
  const content = readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8');
  return content
    .split('\n')
    .filter((line) => line.trim() !== '')
    .map((line) => JSON.parse(line) as NodeRow);
}

  });

describe('frontierOpen veto', () => {
  const composite = (overrides: InputOverrides = {}) => researchInput({
    ratios: [0.5, 0.01, 0.01, 0.01],
    ...overrides,
    config: { depthMode: 'expansive', ...(overrides.config ?? {}) },
    research: { answeredCount: 9, totalQuestions: 10, frontierOpen: 2, ...(overrides.research ?? {}) },
  });

  const cases: Array<[string, InputOverrides, string, string]> = [
    ['open nodes veto the stop', {}, 'CONTINUE', 'frontierOpen'],
    ['an empty frontier lets the stop through', { research: { frontierOpen: 0 } }, 'STOP', 'composite_converged'],
    ['no depth mode leaves the veto off', { config: { depthMode: undefined } }, 'STOP', 'composite_converged'],
    ['the cap still wins', { iterationCount: 10 }, 'STOP', 'maxIterationsReached'],
    ['convergence mode off still wins', { config: { depthMode: 'expansive', convergenceMode: 'off' } }, 'CONTINUE', 'convergenceModeOff'],
    ['max-iterations policy still wins', { config: { depthMode: 'expansive', stopPolicy: 'max-iterations' } }, 'CONTINUE', 'stopPolicyMaxIterations'],
    ['the floor still comes first', { iterationCount: 2 }, 'CONTINUE', 'minIterationsNotReached'],
    ['stuck recovery still comes first', { stuckCount: 3 }, 'STUCK_RECOVERY', 'stuckThresholdReached'],
  ];

  it('the base fixture is a composite_converged stop without the veto', () => {
    const decision = buildStopDecision(composite({ research: { frontierOpen: 0 } }));

    expect(decision.reasonDetail).toBe('composite_converged');
  });

  it.each(cases)('expansive composite candidate: %s', (_label, overrides, decision, reasonDetail) => {
    const result = buildStopDecision(composite(overrides));

    expect(result.decision).toBe(decision);
    expect(result.reasonDetail).toBe(reasonDetail);
  });

  it('never vetoes the all_questions_answered hard path when the frontier is empty', () => {
    const decision = buildStopDecision(researchInput({
      config: { depthMode: 'expansive' },
      research: { frontierOpen: 0 },
    }));

    expect(decision.reasonDetail).toBe('all_questions_answered');
  });

  it('carries depthMode into the resolved config only for expansive runs', () => {
    const graph = { nodes: [], edges: [] };
    const files = { config: { maxIterations: 10 }, registry: {}, stateLogRows: [] };

    const defaultConfig = buildStopDecisionInput({ depthMode: 'default' }, 'research', files, graph).config;
    expect(Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(defaultConfig, 'depthMode')).toBe(false);

    const flagConfig = buildStopDecisionInput({ depthMode: 'expansive' }, 'research', files, graph).config;
    expect(flagConfig.depthMode).toBe('expansive');

    const configConfig = buildStopDecisionInput({}, 'research', {
      config: { maxIterations: 10, antiConvergence: { depthMode: 'expansive' } },
      registry: {},
      stateLogRows: [],
    }, graph).config;
    expect(configConfig.depthMode).toBe('expansive');
  });

  it('projects the open and active frontier nodes into the research facts', () => {
    const graph = { nodes: [], edges: [] };

    const withFrontier = buildStopDecisionInput({ depthMode: 'expansive' }, 'research', {
      config: { maxIterations: 10 },
      registry: { frontier: { nodes: [{ status: 'open' }, { status: 'active' }, { status: 'exhausted' }] } },
      stateLogRows: [],
    }, graph);
    expect(withFrontier.research?.frontierOpen).toBe(2);

    const withoutFrontier = buildStopDecisionInput({ depthMode: 'expansive' }, 'research', {
      config: { maxIterations: 10 },
      registry: {},
      stateLogRows: [],
    }, graph);
    expect(Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(withoutFrontier.research ?? {}, 'frontierOpen')).toBe(false);
  });

  describe('focusAlignment under expansive depth', () => {
    const offFocusRecords = [
      {
        answeredQuestions: ['Which caps does admission read?'],
        keyQuestions: ['What should be checked?'],
      },
    ];

    it('an off-focus frontier answer alone never stops the run', () => {
      const decision = buildStopDecision(
        composite({ research: { frontierOpen: 0, iterationRecords: offFocusRecords } }),
      );

      expect(decision.decision).toBe('CONTINUE');
      expect(decision.reasonDetail).toBe('qualityGuardFailed');
      expect(decision.blockedBy).toEqual(['focusAlignment']);
    });

    it('open frontier nodes continue before the guard is consulted', () => {
      const decision = buildStopDecision(
        composite({ research: { frontierOpen: 2, iterationRecords: offFocusRecords } }),
      );

      expect(decision.decision).toBe('CONTINUE');
      expect(decision.reasonDetail).toBe('frontierOpen');
    });

    it('a frontier answer listed in keyQuestions passes the guard', () => {
      const decision = buildStopDecision(
        composite({
          research: {
            frontierOpen: 0,
            iterationRecords: [
              {
                answeredQuestions: ['Which caps does admission read?'],
                keyQuestions: ['Which caps does admission read?', 'What should be checked?'],
              },
            ],
          },
        }),
      );

      expect(decision.decision).toBe('STOP');
      expect(decision.reasonDetail).toBe('composite_converged');
    });
  });
});

# DR-071 -- Frontier focus picks the shallowest open node

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-071`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the frontier branch of Next Focus. The objective is to confirm that the loop works the shallowest open node first, and that visiting a node does not answer the question it carries.

### Why This Matters

A broad topic needs breadth before depth. If the loop chased a deep node first, it would never cover the depth-one questions. A visit that counted as an answer would also close a question the loop never studied.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the focus choice, the visit rule and the marker that a visit matches.

- Objective: Confirm that the shallowest open node is chosen first, that one visit leaves its question unresolved and that a reworded focus that keeps the node marker still counts as a visit.
- Real user request: Tell me which open sub-question the next iteration works, and show me that a single pass does not mark it answered.
- Prompt: `Check that expansive Next Focus picks the shallowest open frontier node, that one visit with that focus leaves its question open, and that a reworded focus that keeps the node marker still counts as a visit. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the focus case and the reworded-visit case by file stem, then read the marker and the focus line in the reducer.
- Expected signals: The focus case passes and shows a depth-one node chosen over a depth-two node, with its question still open after the visit. The reworded-visit case passes and shows the node spent as `exhausted` with reason `budget` after five visits. The reducer matches the `Frontier node` marker anywhere in the focus and writes the focus as `Frontier node <id> (depth <n>, scope <scope>): <question>`.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can name the node the next iteration works, and can see a visit alone does not close its question.
- Pass/fail: PASS if both cases pass and the reducer shows the marker and the focus line; FAIL if a deeper node is chosen first, a visit answers its question or a reworded visit fails to count.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that expansive Next Focus picks the shallowest open frontier node, that one visit with that focus leaves its question open, and that a reworded focus that keeps the node marker still counts as a visit. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-research-reduce-state.vitest -t "picks the shallowest open node" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-research-reduce-state.vitest -t "counts a visit when the leaf rewords" --reporter=verbose`
3. `rg -n "pickFrontierFocus|Frontier node" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  1 passed | 48 skipped (49)`, with the case `picks the shallowest open node and a visit does not resolve it` passing. Command 2 prints `Tests  1 passed | 48 skipped (49)`, with the case `counts a visit when the leaf rewords the focus around the node marker` passing. Command 3 prints the marker regex, which matches `Frontier node` anywhere in the focus text, and the focus template that `pickFrontierFocus` returns.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary lines from commands 1 and 2, with each exit status.
- The `rg` lines from command 3, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Both cases pass and command 3 shows the marker regex and the focus template.
- **Fail**: A deeper node is chosen first, a visit leaves no trace, or a reworded focus fails to count as a visit.

### Failure Triage

- A deeper node is chosen first: check the ordering in `pickFrontierFocus`, which must sort by depth before the other keys.
- One visit answers its question: check that the frontier focus is not the bare question text.
- A reworded focus does not count: check that the marker regex is not anchored to the start of the focus.

---

# DR-068 -- Expansive depth flag and default byte identity

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-068`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `--depth-mode` flag and its default. The objective is to confirm that a research run without the flag, or with `--depth-mode=default`, produces the same registry, dashboard, Next Focus, prompt pack and stop config as the loop produced before the expansive mode existed.

### Why This Matters

The expansive mode must stay opt-in. An operator who never passes the flag should see no change in what the loop writes or decides. That promise lets the mode ship without a migration.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm that the flag documents `default` as its value when absent and that the golden byte-identity suite passes.

- Objective: Confirm that `--depth-mode` defaults to `default`, that `--depth-mode=expansive` is the only opt-in value and that a default run matches the pre-change baseline on every compared artifact.
- Real user request: Run a deep research session without any new flag and tell me whether anything changed for me.
- Prompt: `Check that --depth-mode defaults to default and that a research run without the flag matches the pre-change baseline byte for byte. Name any byte that differs. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Read the flag row and the config default, read the golden helper that removes one paragraph, then run the golden suite by file stem.
- Expected signals: The flag row lists `default` as the value when the flag is absent. The config carries `"depthMode": "default"`. The golden suite passes 12 of 12 tests. The only paragraph the comparison removes is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a default run matches the baseline byte for byte, except for one expansive-only paragraph in the embedded agent persona of CLI lineage prompts. That exception is a waiver pending operator ratification.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the flag row and the config default both name `default`, the golden suite passes and the helper removes only the persona paragraph; FAIL if any other byte differs or any golden case fails.

- A golden case fails on an artifact other than the lineage prompt: a default path changed. Check the reducer, the stop-decision config echo and the prompt pack in that order, and find the first byte that differs.
- The flag row names a different default: check the research command flag table and the `depthMode` value in the config.
- The helper removes more than the persona paragraph: its pattern no longer matches only that paragraph, so restore the narrow pattern before trusting the verdict.

# DR-073 -- Fan-out deals seeded frontier nodes round-robin

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-073`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates how the expansive frontier crosses a fan-out run. The objective is to confirm that the seeded nodes are dealt across the lineages before any lineage spawns, and that the merge reports a node that no lineage holds.

### Why This Matters

Each lineage works only the nodes it holds. A node that no lineage receives is never worked, and its question stays open for the whole run. The guard makes that loss visible at merge time.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the partition, the store write before spawn, the merge guard and the default path.

- Objective: Confirm that the depth-one nodes are dealt round-robin, that each lineage store exists before its lineage spawns, that the merge guard fails on a missing partitioned node and that a default run writes no lineage store.
- Real user request: Split this research across three lineages, and tell me whether every seeded sub-question reached one of them.
- Prompt: `Check that a fan-out research run under expansive depth mode deals the seeded depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store before spawn, and fails the merge guard when a partitioned node is missing. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the partition, store-before-spawn, default-path and merge-guard cases by file stem, then read the partition helpers in the fan-out runner.
- Expected signals: The round-robin case passes. The store-before-spawn case passes. The default-path case passes and shows no lineage store. The merge guard passes when every partitioned node is reported, and fails when one is missing from every lineage. The runner writes `frontier_scope:` only under the expansive flag.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say each seeded sub-question went to one lineage before spawn, and a missing one would be named at merge.
- Pass/fail: PASS if all four named cases pass and the runner shows the partition and store helpers; FAIL if a seeded node is dealt to no lineage, a store is written after spawn or a default run writes a store.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that a fan-out research run under expansive depth mode deals the seeded depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store before spawn, and fails the merge guard when a partitioned node is missing. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "round-robin" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "writes the lineage store before spawn" --reporter=verbose`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "default depth mode" --reporter=verbose`
4. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-merge.vitest -t "partitioned node" --reporter=verbose`
5. `rg -n "frontier_scope|partitionFrontierNodes|writeLineageFrontierStore" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  1 passed | 202 skipped (203)`, with the case `deals open depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order` passing. Command 2 prints `Tests  1 passed | 202 skipped (203)`, with the case `writes the lineage store before spawn and records the partition` passing. Command 3 prints `Tests  1 passed | 202 skipped (203)`, with the case `a default depth mode writes no lineage store and records no partition` passing. Command 4 prints `Tests  2 passed | 82 skipped (84)`, with the cases `passes the guard when every partitioned node is reported` and `fails the guard when a partitioned node is missing from every lineage` passing. Command 5 prints the partition helper, the store writer and the `frontier_scope` binding.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary lines from commands 1 to 4, with each exit status.
- The `rg` lines from command 5, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: All four commands exit 0 with the expected counts, and command 5 shows the partition and store helpers.
- **Fail**: A seeded node goes to no lineage, a store is written after spawn, a default run writes a store, or the merge guard passes a missing node.

### Failure Triage

- A seeded node goes to no lineage: check the round-robin deal over node-id order in the partition helper.
- A store appears after spawn: check that the store writer runs in the lineage worker before the prompt is built and spawned.
- The merge guard passes a missing node: check that the guard reads the partition recorded in the orchestration summary.

### Workflow Flag Surface

The argument hint names the invocation shape; the whole flag surface is here. Every flag below
is a workflow input, never an execution mode, and the presentation asset owns how each one binds
into `config`.

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default, on the ordinary legal-stop path. `divergent` sends a `composite_converged` or `all_questions_answered` stop into a one-round three-seat Council pivot instead of ending the run, at proportionally higher cost. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the `research/resource-map.md` write; `research.md` still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--restart` | no value | Shorthand that selects `restart`: opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. |
| `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing. `restart` opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_questions_answered` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations` or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`. |
| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
| `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Each occurrence opens a group accepting `--model=X`, `--config-dir=PATH`, `--reasoning-effort=LEVEL`, `--service-tier=TIER`, `--executor-timeout=SECONDS`, `--iters=N`, `--count=N` and `--label=X`. Which of those a given kind supports, and the fail-fast on an unsupported pairing, are in the presentation asset. |
| `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

trigger_phrases:

  - "extend completed review run"
  - "review run_extended"
  - "--extend-minutes review"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Completed-run extension (--extend)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--extend=N` reopens a completed review run in the same session and generation and adds `N` iterations. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event, and keeps the prior review report as `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` before re-synthesis. `--extend-minutes=M` adds minutes to the duration budget of the same extension.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Lineage And Preconditions

The extension uses lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run must already be complete. An unfinished review continues with `--lineage-mode=resume` instead. The extension is refused with fan-out.

### Iteration And Duration Budget

The iteration cap rises by `N` over the prior count. `--extend-minutes=M` is valid only with `--extend`. The new duration budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts time since `createdAt`.

### Stop Interaction

Under `stopPolicy: convergence`, an extension with nothing new can stop again at once on `all_dimensions_clean`. Pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`.

### Ledger And Reducer Routing

The `run_extended` stem is part of the deep-review ledger schema, and the deep-review reducer routes it. `runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` reads the extension through its live lineage modes.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | Flag rows for `--extend` and `--extend-minutes`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Lineage handling and the snapshot of the prior review report. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-review-ledger-schema/deep-review-ledger-schema.ts` | Shared | Defines the `run_extended` stem and its payload. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-review-reducers/deep-review-reducer.ts` | Shared | Routes the `run_extended` stem. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Live lineage modes that read the extension. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-ledger-schema.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Ledger schema coverage for the review lifecycle event stems. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-review-run.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-070: same-session continuation, one `run_extended` event and the report snapshot. |

trigger_phrases:

# Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A review without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under the same caps as research, keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and holds open nodes as `searchDebt`. The live pilot runs that would show this mode on real targets are still open, so this page describes the shipped code and its tests and records no pilot result.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seeding And Admission

The expansive run seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1. Leaf `frontier_proposal` rows are admitted under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps. Merge commits are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff`, and index rebuilds are pruned as `generated-artifact`.

### Dimension Coverage

The active node's next uncovered dimension is dispatched. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so `--max-iterations` should be at least `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` for the depth reached.

### Stop Behavior

A review stop candidate becomes another iteration while nodes stay open. The `frontierOpen` veto applies to the review stop candidates, including `all_dimensions_clean` and the composite stop, after the minIterations floor.

### Pivot

The divergent review pivot runs only once the frontier is terminal. It seeds from terminal unanswered nodes and routes accepted candidates through admission.

### Fan-Out

Fan-out lineages receive a share of the seeded nodes. `runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` returns the merged review frontier and runs the review fan-in guard over the recorded partition.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | The `depth_mode` input, the frontier seed step and the config keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.depthMode`, the `expansiveDepth` block and the locked `depthMode` field. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` | Script | Admits review proposals as node-store rows and open `searchDebt` entries. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Reads the review frontier, the close rule and the per-node dimension cover. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | Applies the frontier-open veto to review stop candidates. |

| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` | Script | Returns the merged review frontier and runs the fan-in guard. |

title: "Review minIterations floor"
description: "A review run with a configured minIterations floor returns CONTINUE with minIterationsNotReached until its iteration count reaches the floor, so a review never stops before the floor."

# Review minIterations floor

## 1. OVERVIEW

The review convergence check holds a stop below the configured floor. `antiConvergence.minIterations` in the review config sets the floor, and the shipped default is 2. The floor applies to research and review alike.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Floor Check

`runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` returns `CONTINUE` with the reason `minIterationsNotReached` while the iteration count is below the floor. A stop candidate below the floor never ends the review.

### Stop Order

The review command's `step_check_convergence` places the floor in the fixed stop order after the candidate line and before the gates, so the floor is checked before any gate can end the run.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | The floor check that returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | The floor's position in the fixed stop order of `step_check_convergence`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.minIterations`, default 2. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/stop-decision.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The review floor case: `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | A configured floor above 2. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-stop-path-replay.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The shipped floor replays unchanged. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/review-min-iterations-floor.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-072: a review run holds below its minIterations floor. |

# Custom review dimensions

## 1. OVERVIEW

A review normally audits four canonical dimensions: correctness, security, traceability and maintainability. A setup can name more. A configured name outside the canonical four is a custom dimension. It is tracked and covered like the others, ordered after the canonical four, and judged by its own name. It is not required for severity coverage.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Setup And Ordering

`.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` accepts custom names at setup, normalizes them, and orders them after the canonical four in the risk order. The review registry seeds the four canonical keys plus the custom keys.

### Coverage Tracking

`runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` derives each finding's dimension by matching it against the configured dimension universe, so a custom name that appears in the finding's focus is kept as its own dimension. When nothing matches, the derivation falls back to the iteration's first addressed dimension and then to `correctness`. `buildDimensionCoverage` builds coverage from the iteration records and the strategy dimensions.

### Review Contract And Agent

`deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` carries a `customDimensionRule`: priority after the canonical four, configured order, judged by its own name under the shared P0/P1/P2 severity doctrine, and `requiredForSeverityCoverage: false`. The review agent definition allows a configured custom dimension under the same rubric rule.

### Stop Behavior

A covered custom dimension counts toward the all-dimensions-clean stop, so a review with every configured dimension covered and clean can reach that stop.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Accepts, normalizes and orders custom names, and seeds the registry keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Derives each finding's dimension against the configured universe and builds dimension coverage. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | The `customDimensionRule` block. |
| `.skilled/agents/deep-review.md` | Agent contract | Allows a configured custom dimension under the custom rubric rule. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The custom dimension stop-path case. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-state-reducer.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Dimension coverage with custom names. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/custom-dimension-setup-and-universe.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-074: a configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/review-depth-v2-rollout/stop-gate-custom-dimension-coverage.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-075: a covered custom dimension reaches the all-dimensions-clean stop. |

# Cross-Mode Anti-Convergence Contract

## 1. OVERVIEW

Documents the cross-mode `antiConvergence` contract as it applies to `deep-review`: a minimum iteration floor, a locked convergence mode, a fail-closed stop policy, runtime capability validation, and optimizer invariants that prevent invalid floor/max-iteration combinations.

For review mode, the shipped config sets `antiConvergence.minIterations` to `2`, `convergenceMode` to `default`, and `stopPolicy` to `fail-closed`. This makes a one-pass STOP illegal at the config layer and keeps optimizer changes from tuning past the floor contract.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The review config declares the floor in `assets/deep-review-config.json`. Its optimizer-managed block marks `antiConvergence.convergenceMode` and `antiConvergence.stopPolicy` as locked fields while keeping the review loop's ordinary tunables (`convergenceThreshold`, `stuckThreshold`, `maxIterations`) separate.

The runtime capability matrix declares `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`. The shared runtime capability resolver rejects any matrix where `stopPolicy` is absent or not `fail-closed`, so callers cannot silently fall back to permissive stop behavior.

The optimizer manifest carries an invariant group for the same contract: `antiConvergence.convergenceMode` and `convergenceMode` stay locked, and `antiConvergence.minIterations` is tunable only when the paired `minIterations <= maxIterations` constraint holds.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | Declares `antiConvergence.minIterations: 2`, `convergenceMode: "default"`, `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`, and locked optimizer-managed fields. |
| `assets/runtime-capabilities.json` | Runtime matrix | Declares the fail-closed stop policy consumed by the runtime capability resolver. |
| `scripts/runtime-capabilities.cjs` | Mode shim | Binds `deep-review` to the shared runtime capability resolver and local matrix path. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/runtime-capabilities.cjs` | Shared runtime | Validates that every loaded matrix includes `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/optimizer/optimizer-manifest.json` | Optimizer | Defines the invariant group that locks convergence mode and rejects `minIterations > maxIterations`. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies the review-facing config, runtime matrix, resolver, and optimizer invariant anchors. |

---

## 4. SOURCE METADATA

- Group: Severity system
- Canonical catalog source: `feature-catalog.md`
- Feature file path: `severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md`
- Primary sources: `assets/deep-review-config.json`, `assets/runtime-capabilities.json`, `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/runtime-capabilities.cjs`, `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/optimizer/optimizer-manifest.json`

# deep-review: Feature Catalog

This document combines the current feature inventory for the `deep-review` system into a single reference. The root catalog acts as the system-level directory: it summarizes the loop lifecycle, state packet, review dimensions, and severity controls, and points to the per-feature files that carry the deeper protocol and source anchors.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

Use this catalog as the canonical inventory for the live `deep-review` feature surface. The numbered sections below group the system by lifecycle, packet state, review coverage, and finding controls so readers can move from a top-level summary into per-feature reference files without losing the contract behind each review phase.

| Category | Coverage | Primary Surfaces |
|---|---:|---|
| Loop lifecycle | 9 features | [`SKILL.md`](../SKILL.md), [`references/protocol/loop-protocol.md`](../references/protocol/loop-protocol.md), deep-review workflows, fan-out runtime primitives |
| State management | 7 features | [`references/state/state-format.md`](../references/state/state-format.md), review packet files, reducer outputs |
| Review dimensions | 4 features | `assets/review-mode-contract.yaml`, [`assets/deep-review-strategy.md`](../assets/deep-review-strategy.md) |
| Severity system | 9 features | [`references/convergence/convergence.md`](../references/convergence/convergence.md), [`references/state/state-format.md`](../references/state/state-format.md), review contract, divergent review pivots |

---

## 2. LOOP LIFECYCLE

These entries cover the end-to-end review loop from spec-folder initialization through dispatch, stop evaluation, synthesis, and continuity save.

### Initialization

#### Description

Creates the canonical review packet and seeds the first review charter.

#### How It Works

Initialization classifies the prior state, resolves the target into files, orders the four review dimensions by risk priority, plans applicable core and overlay traceability protocols, and writes the config, JSONL log, findings registry, and strategy packet under `review/`.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/initialization.md`](loop-lifecycle/initialization.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Iteration dispatch

#### Description

Runs one fresh-context review cycle against the current focus.

#### How It Works

Each loop pass reads the packet state, generates a compact state summary, dispatches `@deep-review` with one focus dimension and file set, requires write-once iteration output plus JSONL and strategy updates, and keeps the agent leaf-only and read-only against the target code.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/iteration-dispatch.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/iteration-dispatch.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Convergence check

#### Description

Determines whether the loop continues, recovers, or stops.

#### How It Works

The stop path combines hard stops, a three-signal weighted vote, blocked-stop persistence, graph-assisted review gates, and stuck recovery. STOP is legal only when convergence math and the review gate bundle agree.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/convergence-check.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/convergence-check.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Synthesis

#### Description

Compiles iteration output into the final review report and terminal state.

#### How It Works

Synthesis deduplicates findings across iteration files, reconciles adjudicated severities, replays the JSONL audit trail, writes the nine-section `review-report.md`, determines PASS or CONDITIONAL or FAIL, and appends the terminal synthesis event.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/synthesis.md`](loop-lifecycle/synthesis.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Memory save

#### Description

Preserves the completed review context for future recovery.

#### How It Works

The save phase treats `generate-context.js` as the supported handoff boundary, keeps the on-disk review packet as ground truth, and does not discard review results when the continuity save step fails.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/memory-save.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/memory-save.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Resource map emission

#### Description

Emits a convergence-time `resource-map.md` from review delta evidence with a clean opt-out path.

#### How It Works

The reducer runs an explicit `--emit-resource-map` pass during synthesis that reads converged delta files and writes `{artifact_dir}/resource-map.md`, grouped by category and annotated with per-file P0/P1/P2 counts. Enabled by default via `config.resource_map.emit = true`. Operators can disable per run with `--no-resource-map`.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/resource-map-emission.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/resource-map-emission.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Resource Map Coverage Gate

#### Description

Reports how well the review covered the artifacts declared in the spec folder's `resource-map.md` before a verdict is finalized.

#### How It Works

A conditional synthesis section emitted only when `config.resource_map_present == true`, inserted as section 8 of the review report. It reports touched entries, untouched entries (`expected-by-scope` vs `gap`), and implementation paths absent from the map. It is descriptive and does not by itself block STOP.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/resource-map-coverage-gate.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/resource-map-coverage-gate.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Executor selection contract

#### Description

Resolves which executor runs each iteration and enforces per-kind flag compatibility before dispatch.

#### How It Works

`parseExecutorConfig` resolves `config.executor.kind` into its dispatch branch: `native` dispatches the agent, every CLI kind but `cli-mistral-vibe` runs through `dispatch-iteration.cjs`, and `cli-mistral-vibe` runs only as a fan-out lineage. Unsupported flags throw `ExecutorConfigError` at parse time. All branches share prompt rendering, output validation, and executor audit append.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/executor-selection-contract.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/executor-selection-contract.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Fan-out loop dispatch

#### Description

Opt-in fan-out dispatch layer: `step_resolve_artifact_root` artifact-dir override branch,
`step_fanout_spawn` (CLI pool + native sequential agent dispatch), and `step_fanout_merge`
with strongest-restriction verdict binding at the top of `phase_synthesis`. Single-executor
path is byte-identical to pre-change behavior.

#### How It Works

Three new YAML steps gated on `config.fanout` presence. CLI lineages run via `fanout-run.cjs`
(pool-capped headless subprocesses). Native lineages run as sequential `agent: deep-review`
dispatches. `step_fanout_merge` produces a consolidated `deep-review-findings-registry.json`
using strongest-restriction (any active P0 → `mergedVerdict=FAIL`) and binds
`p0_count/p1_count/p2_count` from the merged result for `step_derive_verdict`. This
binding is review-specific: without it, `step_derive_verdict` would read zero counts from an
empty single-executor state log. Command flags: `--executor` (repeatable), `--executors
<json>`, `--concurrency N`. Review verdict: strongest-restriction across all lineages.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/fanout-dispatch.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/fanout-dispatch.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

function buildRegistry(strategyDimensions, iterationFiles, iterationRecords, config, corruptionWarnings = [], deltaRecords = [], eventRecords = iterationRecords, frontierStore = null) {
  const terminalStop = buildTerminalStopState(iterationRecords);
  const lineage = buildLineageState(config, iterationRecords, terminalStop);
  const dimensionUniverse = buildDimensionUniverse(config);
  const { openFindings, resolvedFindings } = buildFindingRegistry(iterationFiles, iterationRecords, deltaRecords, dimensionUniverse);
  const dimensionCoverage = buildDimensionCoverage(iterationRecords, strategyDimensions, dimensionUniverse);
  const findingsBySeverity = buildFindingsBySeverity(openFindings);
  const convergenceScore = computeConvergenceScore(iterationRecords);
  const graphConvergence = buildGraphConvergenceRollup(iterationRecords);
  const blockedStopHistory = buildBlockedStopHistory(iterationRecords);
  const terminalGates = buildTerminalGates(iterationRecords);
  const searchLedgerState = buildSearchLedgerState(iterationRecords);
  // The frontier store is this run's own queue, so its projection is opt-in with
  // the depth mode that fills it: a default run never reads the store and its
  // registry keeps the shape it had before the mode existed.
  const frontier = config?.antiConvergence?.depthMode === 'expansive'
    ? buildReviewFrontier(
      frontierStore && typeof frontierStore === 'object' ? frontierStore : { nodes: [], history: [] },
      iterationRecords,
      config,
    )
    : null;
  if (frontier) mergeFrontierIntoSearchLedger(searchLedgerState, frontier);
  const traceability = buildTraceabilityRollup(iterationRecords);
  traceability.unfinishedCoreProtocols = reviewUnfinishedCoreProtocols(config, { traceability });
  const divergence = buildPivotState(eventRecords);
  const fieldWarnings = validateReviewRecordFields(iterationRecords);
  const status = deriveDashboardStatus(config, iterationRecords, terminalStop);

  // Split repeatedFindings into two semantically distinct buckets
  // so persistent-same-severity findings and severity-churn findings don't collapse.
  const persistentSameSeverity = openFindings.filter((finding) => {
    if (finding.lastSeen - finding.firstSeen < 1) return false;
    const transitions = Array.isArray(finding.transitions) ? finding.transitions : [];
    // Exclude the initial discovery transition and any transition that records
    // the same severity on both sides, which a refinement can add without churn.
    const nontrivialTransitions = transitions.filter((t) => t.from !== null && t.from !== t.to);
    return nontrivialTransitions.length === 0;
  });

    const protocolId = normalizeText(entry?.protocolId) || normalizeText(entry?.protocol);
    if (!protocolId) continue;
    const result = traceabilityResult(protocolId, entry);
    if (result) normalized.push(result);
  }
  // A protocol map is any object whose values are status strings or result
  // objects; its bookkeeping keys are named above and never read as protocols.
  for (const map of [source, source.core, source.overlay]) {
    if (map === null || typeof map !== 'object' || Array.isArray(map)) continue;
    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(map)) {
      if (TRACEABILITY_META_KEYS.has(key)) continue;
      const isStatusObject = value !== null && typeof value === 'object'
        && !Array.isArray(value) && typeof value.status === 'string';
      if (typeof value !== 'string' && !isStatusObject) continue;
      const result = traceabilityResult(key, value);
      if (result) normalized.push(result);
    }
  }
  return normalized;
}

/**
 * Whether a result carries an evidence reference that names a file and line.
 *
 * A `partial` verdict records a claim that did not resolve, so a later `pass`
 * only supersedes it when it points at where the claim now resolves. The
 * reference is read from the result's evidence fields, which a record may hold
 * as one string or as a list.
 *
 * @param {*} result - Normalized protocol result.
 * @returns {boolean} True when an evidence field holds a file:line reference.
 */
function hasTraceabilityFileLineReference(result) {
  for (const field of [result?.evidenceRefs, result?.evidence]) {
    const values = Array.isArray(field) ? field : [field];
    for (const value of values) {
      if (typeof value === 'string' && TRACEABILITY_FILE_LINE_REFERENCE.test(value)) return true;
    }
  }
  return false;
}

/**
 * Aggregate the latest result for each traceability protocol into the registry.
 * Iterations may report only the protocols exercised in that pass.
 */
function buildTraceabilityRollup(iterationRecords) {
  const latestByProtocol = new Map();
  for (const record of Array.isArray(iterationRecords) ? iterationRecords : []) {
    for (const result of normalizeTraceabilityChecks(record?.traceabilityChecks)) {
      const previous = latestByProtocol.get(result.protocolId);

# Convergence check

## 1. OVERVIEW

Determines whether the review loop continues, enters recovery, or stops legally.

This phase evaluates the review history after every iteration. It mixes hard stops, a weighted stop vote, legal-stop gates, graph-aware blockers, pause handling, and stuck recovery so the loop only exits when the review is actually saturated.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The live convergence model evaluates outcomes in priority order: max iterations, all dimensions covered with stabilization, stuck detection, composite convergence, legal-stop gate bundle, then default continue. The weighted vote uses rolling average, MAD noise floor, and dimension coverage as its three core signals. A new P0 finding forces `newFindingsRatio` up to at least `0.50`, which blocks premature stop.

Even when the weighted vote requests STOP, the loop still has to pass the review gate bundle. Failed gates persist a first-class `blocked_stop` event with `blockedBy`, `gateResults`, and `recoveryStrategy`, then continue the loop. The same stop path can be vetoed by graph convergence or by a failed claim adjudication packet, and stuck recovery pivots toward the least-covered dimension instead of repeating the same exhausted pass.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `references/convergence/convergence.md` | Protocol | Defines hard stops, stuck detection, composite vote, legal-stop gates, recovery strategies, and convergence reporting. |
| `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` | Protocol | Wires convergence, pause sentinel checks, blocked-stop persistence, and loop-decision behavior into the lifecycle. |
| `references/state/state-format.md` | Schema | Defines blocked-stop, graph-convergence, pause, recovery, and synthesis event shapes in JSONL. |
| `assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | Declares convergence defaults, severity math, quality gates, verdicts, and release-readiness states. |

### Validation And Tests

|---|---|---|

| `manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/composite-review-convergence-stop-behavior.md` | Manual playbook | Exercises the weighted stop vote. |
| `manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/stuck-recovery-widens-dimension-focus.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies least-covered-dimension recovery behavior. |
| `manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/graph-convergence-review.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies graph-assisted convergence and blockers. |
| `manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/blocked-stop-reducer-surfacing.md` | Manual playbook | Confirms blocked-stop events surface into reducer-owned state. |

# Initialization

## 1. OVERVIEW

Creates the canonical deep-review packet and seeds the first review charter.

This phase turns a requested review target into a working `review/` packet. It decides whether the run is fresh or resumable, resolves scope, orders review dimensions, selects applicable traceability protocols, and writes the initial packet files the loop depends on.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

Initialization begins by classifying the prior state as `fresh`, `resume`, `completed-session`, or `invalid-state`. A valid new run creates `review/iterations/`, resolves the target according to one of the five target types, and writes the canonical packet under `{spec_folder}/review/`.

The initializer also applies the live four-dimension priority order: correctness first, security second, traceability third, maintainability fourth. It creates `deep-review-config.json`, seeds the first `deep-review-state.jsonl` config record, creates an empty reducer-owned findings registry, and populates `deep-review-strategy.md` with topic, dimensions, files under review, cross-reference status, known context, and review boundaries.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `SKILL.md` | Skill contract | Describes the 3-layer architecture, packet location, lifecycle modes, and required packet files. |
| `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` | Protocol | Defines Phase 1 initialization, target resolution, dimension ordering, protocol planning, and init outputs. |
| `references/state/state-format.md` | Schema | Defines the config file fields, initial JSONL config record, registry shape, and file protection rules. |
| `assets/deep-review-strategy.md` | Template | Supplies the strategy sections populated during initialization. |
| `assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | Declares target types, dimensions, lineage fields, outputs, and reducer IO. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|

| `manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/invalid-or-contradictory-review-state-halts-for-repair.md` | Manual playbook | Confirms contradictory state halts instead of being guessed into shape. |
| `manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/scope-discovery-and-dimension-ordering.md` | Manual playbook | Checks target resolution, dimension ordering, and initial strategy content. |

# DRV-080 -- Frontier open veto holds a review stop, hard stops included

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-080`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `frontierOpen` veto in the review stop decision for `DRV-080`. The objective is to confirm that a review stop waits while frontier nodes stay open, that the veto covers the clean stop that counts as a hard stop, and that every hard limit keeps its precedence.

### Why This Matters

A review that has checked every configured dimension looks finished. The veto keeps the review going while a sub-target is still open. It must never outrank a hard limit, or a review could loop past its budget. It must also cover the clean stop, or the veto would hold back only the soft stops.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the commands below and confirm the review veto table and the guard line that makes review candidates vetoable.

- Objective: Confirm that an open node vetoes a review stop candidate only under the expansive mode, that the veto covers `all_dimensions_clean`, which is a hard stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy keep their own decisions.
- Real user request: The review said it was clean, but it kept going. Why, and do the hard limits still stop it on time?
- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a review stop candidate continues while frontier nodes are open, including the clean stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the review veto table and the composite case by file stem, read the guard line that marks review candidates vetoable, then read the veto branch that sits after the floor check.
- Expected signals: All eight table rows pass. The row for open nodes returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen` for the clean stop. The row for an empty frontier returns `STOP` with `all_dimensions_clean`. The cap, floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and max-iterations rows keep their own decisions. The composite case returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen` while nodes are open and `STOP` with `composite_converged` once they drain. The guard line makes every review candidate vetoable.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a review waits for its open sub-targets, clean stop included, and the hard limits still stop the run on time.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the table, the composite case and the guard line all pass or print as described; FAIL if an open node lets a review stop through, or a hard limit loses to the veto.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a review stop candidate continues while frontier nodes are open, including the clean stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision.vitest -t "expansive review candidate|vetoes a composite review stop while the frontier is open" --reporter=verbose`
2. `rg -n -F "loopType === 'review' || !candidate.hardStop" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  9 passed | 83 skipped (92)`. The eight table rows are named `expansive review candidate: <row label>`, and the composite case is `vetoes a composite review stop while the frontier is open`. The row `open nodes veto the clean stop` returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`, and the row `an empty frontier lets the clean stop through` returns `STOP` with `all_dimensions_clean`. Command 2 prints one line, 1191, which sets `vetoable` to true for every review loop and for any research candidate that is not a hard stop.

The table covers the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy. It does not cover consecutive failures or pause, so this scenario makes no claim about those two.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary line from command 1, with its exit status.
- The line from command 2, read from the current `convergence.cjs`.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Command 1 exits 0 with its nine cases passing, and command 2 prints the guard line.
- **Fail**: An open node lets a review stop through, the clean stop escapes the veto, a hard limit loses to the veto, or command 2 prints nothing.

### Failure Triage

1. An open node lets a review stop through: check that the veto reads the expansive flag and the count of open or active nodes from the review facts.
2. The clean stop escapes the veto: check the guard on line 1191. A review loop must stay vetoable whatever the candidate's `hardStop` value.
3. A hard limit loses to the veto: check the order of the veto and the cap, floor and stuck checks in the decision, since the veto must come after the floor.

# DRV-081 -- Review pivot waits for a terminal frontier

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-081`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the review divergent pivot gate for `DRV-081`. It focuses on the refusal while a frontier node is open, on the revisit directions the pivot seeds from sub-targets the frontier closed without covering every dimension, and on the default run, which keeps the pivot exactly as before.

### Why This Matters

The pivot reopens the review from a new angle. If it ran while a sub-target was still open, it would compete with the frontier for the same ground. If it ignored the sub-targets the frontier gave up on, those sub-targets would never get a second look.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say the pivot waited until the frontier closed, and each sub-target the frontier gave up on got one revisit direction.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the pivot cases, the gate case and the seed label all pass or print; FAIL if the pivot runs while a node is open, an answered sub-target gets a revisit, or a default run seeds a frontier direction.
