# DRV-072 -- Review run holds below its minIterations floor

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-072`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the minimum-iteration floor for `DRV-072`. It focuses on a review run whose stop candidate arrives before its configured `minIterations`: the convergence check must return `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached`, and the run may release only once the floor is reached.

### Why This Matters

The floor used to apply to research runs only. A review that reached a stop candidate at iteration 2 with a floor of 3 could stop one iteration early, before the reviewer had the second full pass the floor exists to guarantee. The shipped floor of 2 is unchanged, so only an operator who sets a higher floor sees the change.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a single check of the convergence decision with a floor set above the stop candidate, so the outcome comes from the floor and not from the default.

- Objective: Confirm that a review stop candidate below `minIterations` returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`, and that the same run releases at the floor.
- Real user request: I set a review to run at least three iterations. Do not let it stop at two, even if it looks converged.
- Prompt: `Stop a deep-review run that reaches a stop candidate at iteration 2 with minIterations set to 3, and confirm the run continues with minIterationsNotReached and releases only at iteration 3.`
- Expected execution process: Read the floor check in the convergence script, read the stop-order item in the review workflow, then run the convergence and stop-decision tests that drive a real review loop state through the floor.
- Expected signals: The floor check returns `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` at iteration 2, the same loop state releases at iteration 3, the shipped floor of 2 still passes its replay, the review workflow names the floor as stop-order item `4a`, and the stop-decision case reads that a review run gets the floor.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told the review will not stop before its configured minimum, and why it continued.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the iteration-2 candidate continues with `minIterationsNotReached`, the iteration-3 candidate releases, and the replay and parity suites pass; FAIL if a review releases below its floor or the floor applies to research only.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Stop a deep-review run that reaches a stop candidate at iteration 2 with minIterations set to 3, and confirm the run continues with minIterationsNotReached and releases only at iteration 3.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n 'minIterationsNotReached' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`
2. `rg -n '4a\. if iteration_count' .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run review-depth-convergence.vitest stop-decision.vitest review-stop-path-replay.vitest stop-policy-yaml-parity.vitest`

### Expected

Command 1 prints the floor's `CONTINUE` return with `minIterationsNotReached`. Command 2 prints stop-order item `4a`. Command 3 exits 0 with no failed test. The `review-depth-convergence` case shows `CONTINUE` at iteration 2 and a release at iteration 3 from the same loop state.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 and 2 matches, and the command 3 summary with the named cases.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The iteration-2 candidate returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`, the iteration-3 candidate releases, the replay still passes at the shipped floor, and the YAML parity suite passes.
- **Fail**: A review releases below its floor, the reason is missing or wrong, the replay fails at the shipped floor, or the stop-order item is absent from the workflow.

### Failure Triage

If the run releases at iteration 2, check that the floor check in `convergence.cjs` runs on the candidate before the legal-stop gates and no longer depends on the loop type. If the shipped replay fails, the floor value has changed, since the shipped floor of 2 must stay as it is.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Shipped floor: run the stop-path replay with its fixtures unchanged and confirm the outcome matches its recorded expectation.

      step_check_convergence:
        action: "Branch on the stop decision that convergence.cjs computed in step_graph_convergence"
        algorithm: |
          The whole stop decision is computed by convergence.cjs from the review config, the state log, the findings registry and the coverage graph. This step performs no arithmetic and never recomputes or overrides the result.
          The ratio signals leave out thought iterations and every iteration whose status is error or timeout, and stuck_count is loop_counters_json.stuckCount.
          The script applies this fixed order, first match wins:
          1. if iteration_count >= max_iterations: STOP (maxIterationsReached). This terminal cap is evaluated before every veto, the claim-adjudication veto included; failed legal-stop gates are recorded in stop_decision.blockedBy as synthesis evidence.
          1a. After 3 consecutive failed iterations (status error or timeout), halt the loop and enter synthesis with stop reason error and partial findings.
          2. if stuck_count >= config.stuckThreshold: STUCK_RECOVERY
          3. if convergence_mode == "off": skip convergence stop candidates and continue.
          3a. if stop_policy == "max-iterations" AND iteration_count < max_iterations: the vote is telemetry only and the decision is CONTINUE.
          4. a STOP candidate exists when every dimension is clean (all_dimensions_clean) or the weighted vote passes (composite_converged). The vote cutoffs follow the configured convergenceThreshold.
          4a. if iteration_count < min_iterations: CONTINUE (minIterationsNotReached). min_iterations is config.minIterations, else antiConvergence.minIterations, capped at max_iterations; like research, the floor runs on a candidate before the legal-stop gates.
          5. the nine legal-stop gates run on a candidate: convergenceGate, dimensionCoverageGate, p0ResolutionGate, evidenceDensityGate, hotspotSaturationGate, claimAdjudicationGate, fixCompletenessReplayGate, candidateCoverageGate, graphlessFallbackGate. Any failed gate gives BLOCKED (blockedStop).
          6. the graph decision settles the candidate: STOP_ALLOWED keeps STOP, STOP_BLOCKED gives BLOCKED, CONTINUE or a missing graph decision gives CONTINUE.
          Bind the outputs from stop_decision, where G is stop_decision.gates and I is stop_decision.inputs.review:
            decision = stop_decision.decision
            stopReason = stop_decision.stopReason
            reason = stop_decision.stopReason when it is not null, else stop_decision.reasonDetail
            reason_detail = stop_decision.reasonDetail
            weighted_stop_score = stop_decision.weightedStopScore
            stop_blocked = (stop_decision.decision == "BLOCKED")
            blocked_by_json = stop_decision.blockedBy
            blocked_by_csv = the members of stop_decision.blockedBy joined with commas
            convergence_gate_pass = G.convergenceGate.pass
            convergence_gate_score = G.convergenceGate.score
            dimension_coverage_gate_pass = G.dimensionCoverageGate.pass
            dimension_coverage_gate_missing_json = G.dimensionCoverageGate.missing
            dimension_coverage_gate_covered_json = the configured review dimensions that are not in G.dimensionCoverageGate.missing
            p0_resolution_gate_pass = G.p0ResolutionGate.pass
            active_p0_count = G.p0ResolutionGate.activeP0
            evidence_density_gate_pass = G.evidenceDensityGate.pass
            avg_evidence_per_finding = I.evidenceBackedP0P1, the count of active P0 and P1 findings that carry file:line evidence
            hotspot_saturation_gate_pass = G.hotspotSaturationGate.pass

# Deep Research Strategy - Session Tracking Template

Runtime template copied to `{spec_folder}/research/` during initialization. Tracks research progress across iterations.

## 1. OVERVIEW

### Purpose

Serves as the "persistent brain" for a deep research session. Records what to investigate, what worked, what failed, and where to focus next. Read by the orchestrator and agents at every iteration.

### Usage

- **Init:** Orchestrator copies this template to `{spec_folder}/research/deep-research-strategy.md` and populates Topic, Key Questions, Known Context, and Research Boundaries from config and memory context.
- **Per iteration:** Agent reads Next Focus, writes iteration evidence, and the reducer refreshes What Worked/Failed, answered questions, carried-forward questions, ruled-out directions, and Next Focus.
- **Mutability:** Mutable — analyst-owned sections remain stable, while machine-owned sections are rewritten by the reducer after each iteration. Section 3 is a generated projection from the reducer registry.
- **Protection:** Shared state with explicit ownership boundaries. Orchestrator validates consistency on resume.

### Question Injection Surface

Use `{spec_folder}/research/inbox.jsonl` to append external questions during an active run. Each line is one JSON object with:

- `id`: stable inbox record identifier
- `text`: question text to promote
- `source`: concrete source label, such as an angle bank entry, analyst strategy, or operator note
- `origin`: one of `angle-bank`, `analyst-strategy`, `operator`, or `legacy-import`
- `injectedAtIteration`: iteration number when the question was introduced
- `promotedQuestionId`: promoted registry question id, or `null` until promotion

The reducer reads the inbox on every reduce step and carries `origin` into the question registry and dashboard badges. Direct edits to Section 3 still work as a compatibility path, but they are attributed as `legacy-import`.

Question ownership is explicit:

- Inbox rows are immutable input.
- The reducer registry is canonical question state.
- Section 3 is rendered only from the registry view.

When an inbox row targets an existing registry question but carries different text, the reducer keeps the registry value, records `operatorDecision: needs_decision`, and appends a `question_conflict` event with both `inboxValue` and `registryValue`.

### Bounded Context Snapshot

Populate during initialization when the target is codebase-scoped. Keep this pointer-based and small:

- Source pointers: paths, symbols, or resource-map entries relevant to the topic.
- Reuse candidates: existing utilities, patterns, docs, or agents worth extending.
- Integration points: files or contracts the research is likely to touch.
- Constraints and risks: scope limits, stale graph or memory gaps, and known non-goals.

Do not inline full source bodies. Do not dispatch the retired standalone context loop. Use `@context` for one-shot retrieval, and use this snapshot only to seed the research loop.

//             (strongest-restriction: any lineage P0 → merged FAIL) +
//             fanout-attribution.md

'use strict';

const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const { spawnSync } = require('node:child_process');
const {
  parseIterationMarkdownFindings,
  latestIterationRecords,
  deltaRowIteration,
  findingKeys,
} = require('../lib/deep-loop/iteration-findings.cjs');
const { assertKnownFlags } = require('./lib/cli-guards.cjs');
const {
  activeReviewSeverityCounts,
  deriveReviewVerdict,
  lineageFailedRequiredGateIds,
  openSearchDebtCount,
} = require('./review-verdict.cjs');

const SEVERITY_RANK = { P0: 3, P1: 2, P2: 1 };

// Every option the CLI merge entrypoint reads. parseArgs accepts any --key,
// so without this list a misspelled flag would be silently ignored and change
// what the merge does.
const MERGE_FLAGS = ['loopType', 'artifactDir', 'enableNearDuplicateDedup'];

/**
 * Collects findings whose `severity` is outside the three-tier scale of SEVERITY_RANK.
 *
 * The review scale has exactly three tiers, so no value outside it carries a rank: the merge
 * would sort it below every P2 and let it raise nothing, and a finding that raises nothing is
 * indistinguishable from a finding nobody wrote. Only the producer can fix the word it stamped,
 * so the value is reported and attributed instead of absorbed -- the merge cannot invent a tier
 * the scale does not have, and guessing one would hide the defect a second way.
 */
function collectUnrankedSeverities(findings, lineage, out) {
  for (const finding of findings || []) {
    const severity = finding && finding.severity;
    // An absent severity is a different defect and the registry-shape warnings already cover a
    // missing field, so only a present-but-unranked value is reported here.
    if (severity === undefined || severity === null || severity === '') continue;
    if (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(SEVERITY_RANK, severity)) continue;
    const findingId = finding.findingId || finding.id || finding.title || '(unidentified)';
    const alreadyReported = out.some(
      (entry) => entry.lineage === lineage && entry.findingId === findingId && entry.severity === severity,
    );
    if (alreadyReported) continue;
    out.push({ severity: String(severity), lineage, findingId });
  }
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. TSX BOOTSTRAP
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const TSX_LOADER = require.resolve('tsx');

// The merged registry and attribution share the runtime's atomic-state helpers,
// which are TypeScript ESM. Re-exec once under the tsx loader so the dynamic
// import below resolves them; mirrors convergence.cjs. Only the CLI entrypoint
// re-execs — module consumers (unit tests) import the pure helpers directly.
if (require.main === module && process.env.DEEP_LOOP_TSX_LOADED !== '1') {
  const child = spawnSync(
    process.execPath,
    ['--import', TSX_LOADER, __filename, ...process.argv.slice(2)],
    {
      cwd: process.cwd(),
      env: require('./runtime-bootstrap.cjs').tsxChildEnv({ DEEP_LOOP_TSX_LOADED: '1' }),
      encoding: 'utf8',
    },
  );
  if (child.stdout) process.stdout.write(child.stdout);
  if (child.stderr) process.stderr.write(child.stderr);
  process.exit(child.status === null ? 1 : child.status);
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. HELPERS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function inputError(message) {
  const err = new Error(message);
  err.code = 'INPUT_VALIDATION';
  return err;
}

function jsonOut(payload) {
  process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(payload)}\n`);
}

function parseArgs(argv = process.argv.slice(2)) {
  const args = {};
  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i += 1) {
    const token = argv[i];
    if (!token.startsWith('--')) {
      throw inputError(`Unexpected positional argument: ${token}`);
    }
    const key = token.slice(2).replace(/-([a-z])/g, (_, c) => c.toUpperCase());
    const next = argv[i + 1];
    if (next === undefined || next.startsWith('--')) {
      args[key] = true;
    } else {
      args[key] = next;
      i += 1;

```

**Recovery evaluation**:
- If recovery iteration finds any new P0/P1 or materially advances required traceability coverage: recovery successful. Reset stuck count. Continue.
- If recovery iteration finds only P2 or nothing: recovery failed. If all dimensions are already covered, exit to synthesis. Otherwise, move to the next unreviewed dimension.

---

### Expansive Depth Frontier (opt-in)

Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, set to `maxIterations` at config creation. A run without the flag keeps the default path and runs exactly as before, and both the mode and the caps are locked against the optimizer.

`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the review target into depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. A merge that carries no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`, because neither is reviewable work.

`step_read_state` reads `active_frontier_node` from `registry.frontier.activeNodeId` and sets `next_dimension` to that node's next uncovered dimension, so the iteration reviews depth the node has not spent yet. `step_frontier_activate` marks the node `active` before the dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node; a leaf proposes `frontier_proposal` rows only when its block is non-empty.

`step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. Relevance includes a node's scope sitting inside a file-list review target. The first failure prunes the proposal under that check's name. A review run has no inbox, so the store alone queues an admitted node, and `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs --target review` is its only writer.

Next Focus reads, in precedence order: blocked-stop recovery, the pivot, the active frontier node, the leaf's `nextFocus`, then the first uncovered dimension. The `frontierOpen` veto holds the loop at CONTINUE while a node stays open, and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor. Stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted` with reason `stuck` before recovery continues.

On a fan-out run the depth-1 nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order, `frontier_scope:` binds each lineage's node ids beside `review_focus:` in its prompt, and the partition is recorded as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. Each lineage's store is written before it spawns, the merge returns a merged `frontier`, and a partition guard names any dealt node no lineage reports. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so 50% of each lineage's `maxIterations` should reach `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`; below that the depth-1 band cannot fund the nodes it was dealt.

---

## 4. PHASE 3: SYNTHESIS

### Purpose

Compile all iteration findings into the final `{artifact_dir}/review-report.md`. The synthesis phase owns the canonical review report output.

### Steps

#### Step 1: Finding Registry Deduplication

Consolidate findings across all iterations:

1. Read all iteration files: `{artifact_dir}/iterations/iteration-*.md`
2. Group findings by file + line range + root cause
3. Merge duplicates, keeping the highest severity and all evidence
4. Assign final finding IDs: `F001`, `F002`, ...
5. Preserve the audit trail: note which iterations contributed to each merged finding

#### Step 2: Severity Reconciliation

Use adjudicated `finalSeverity` for any P0/P1 that was downgraded during claim adjudication (Step 4a of the iteration loop). The original severity from the iteration file is preserved in the audit appendix.

#### Step 3: Replay Validation

Recompute the convergence outcome from JSONL state before finalizing the report:

1. Read `deep-review-state.jsonl` and select review-mode iteration records in run order
2. Recompute `newFindingsRatio`, rolling-average votes, MAD votes, and coverage votes from stored JSONL fields only

# DRV-076 -- Expansive depth flag and default byte identity

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-076`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `--depth-mode` flag on `/deep:review` and its default. The objective is to confirm that a review run without the flag, or with `--depth-mode=default`, produces the same registry, dashboard, Next Focus, prompt pack, pivot seeds, lineage prompt and stop config as the loop produced before the expansive mode existed.

### Why This Matters

The expansive mode must stay opt-in. An operator who never passes the flag should see no change in what the review writes or decides. That promise lets the mode ship without a migration of existing review runs.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the commands below and confirm that the flag documents `default` as its value when absent and that the golden byte-identity suite passes.

- Objective: Confirm that `--depth-mode` defaults to `default`, that `--depth-mode=expansive` is the only opt-in value and that a default review run matches the pre-change baseline on every compared artifact.
- Real user request: Run a deep review without any new flag and tell me whether anything changed for me.
- Prompt: `Check that --depth-mode defaults to default and that a review run without the flag matches the pre-change baseline byte for byte, except for the expansive-only agent paragraph. Name any byte that differs. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Read the flag row and the config default, read the golden helper that removes one paragraph, then run the golden suite by file stem.
- Expected signals: The flag row lists `default` as the value when the flag is absent. The config carries `"depthMode": "default"` under `antiConvergence`. The golden suite passes 14 of 14 cases, seven artifacts for each of two variants: the absent key and an explicit `default`. The only text the comparison removes is the expansive-only paragraph of the review persona in the CLI lineage prompt.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a default review run matches the baseline byte for byte, except for one expansive-only paragraph in the embedded agent persona of CLI lineage prompts. That paragraph is waived pending operator ratification, and the comparison strips it.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the flag row and the config default both name `default`, the golden suite passes with no failed count and the helper removes only the persona paragraph; FAIL if any other byte differs or any golden case fails.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that --depth-mode defaults to default and that a review run without the flag matches the pre-change baseline byte for byte, except for the expansive-only agent paragraph. Name any byte that differs. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n -- '--depth-mode' .skilled/commands/deep/review.md`
2. `rg -n '"depthMode"' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json`
3. `rg -n --glob 'review-depth-mode-golden.vitest.ts' 'withoutExpansiveOnlyPersona' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`
4. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run review-depth-mode-golden.vitest`

### Expected

Command 1 prints one flag row. Its value column reads `default`, the value when the flag is absent. Command 2 prints `"depthMode": "default"`. Command 3 prints three lines: a header comment that points at the helper, the helper definition, and its one call, which applies to the CLI lineage prompt. Command 4 prints `Tests  14 passed (14)` with no failed count. The seven compared artifacts are `registry.json`, `dashboard.md`, `next-focus.txt`, `prompt-pack.md`, `pivot-seeds.json`, `lineage-prompt.txt` and `stop-decision-config.json`, and each matches its baseline.

### Evidence

- The flag row from command 1 and the config line from command 2.
- The `rg` lines from command 3, and the vitest summary line and exit status from command 4.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The flag row names `default` as the value when the flag is absent, the config carries `"depthMode": "default"` and the golden suite passes with no failed count.
- **Fail**: The flag row or the config names a different default, a golden case fails, or the helper removes anything other than the expansive-only persona paragraph.

### Failure Triage

1. A golden case fails on an artifact other than the lineage prompt: a default path changed. Check the reducer output, the stop-decision config echo and the prompt pack in that order, and find the first byte that differs.
2. The flag row names a different default: check the review command flag table and the `depthMode` value under `antiConvergence` in the config.
3. The helper removes more than the persona paragraph: its pattern no longer matches only that paragraph, so restore the narrow pattern before trusting the verdict.

title: "DRV-070 -- Completed-run extension with --extend"

# DRV-070 -- Completed-run extension with --extend

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-070`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates completed-run extension for `DRV-070`. It focuses on `--extend=N` reopening a completed review run in the same session and generation, recording the extension as one `run_extended` event, and keeping the prior review report as a versioned snapshot before the run re-synthesizes.

### Why This Matters

A review that stopped at its iteration cap often has one more dimension worth checking. The operator should be able to add N iterations to that same review without a new session. If the extension resets the generation, drops the prior report, or appends a second extension event, the review history no longer shows what was checked and when.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a real orchestrator-led check against a completed fixture review rather than a synthetic matrix. The scenario is only complete when the operator can explain, in plain language, how many iterations the extension added and where the prior report was kept.

- Objective: Verify that `--extend=3` on a completed review run adds exactly three iterations in the same session and generation, records one `run_extended` event, and snapshots the prior review report.
- Real user request: The review finished at its iteration cap, and one dimension still has open questions. Give the same review three more iterations.
- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-review run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the review report snapshot.`
- Expected execution process: Read the completed fixture config under `{artifact_dir}`, run the review command with `--extend=3` against that spec folder, then read the state log, the config and the snapshot after the run finishes.
- Expected signals: Exactly one `run_extended` event appears, projected as one `extended` row in `deep-review-state.jsonl`. The config keeps the original `sessionId` and generation 1, `maxIterations` equals the prior iteration count plus 3, and the lineage mode is `completed-continue`. No `resumed` row is written for the extension. A file matching `review-report-v*-r*.md` holds the prior report, and exactly three new iteration files are written under `iterations/`.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told the review was extended by three iterations, that the same session continues, and where the prior report was kept.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the event count, the session identifier, the iteration cap, the snapshot and the iteration count all match the expected signals; FAIL if any one of them differs, including a second `run_extended` event or a missing snapshot.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-review run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the review report snapshot.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n -- '--extend' .skilled/commands/deep/review.md .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml`
2. `rg -n 'run_extended' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run append-mode-event-legacy-seam.vitest workflow-restart-lifecycle.vitest`
4. `/deep:review "<fixture target>" :auto --spec-folder=<completed-fixture-spec-folder> --extend=3`
5. `rg -c '"event":"extended"' <artifact_dir>/deep-review-state.jsonl` (the `run_extended` stem lives in the ledger, and the state log carries its projected `extended` row)
6. `jq '{sessionId, generation, maxIterations, lineageMode}' <artifact_dir>/deep-review-config.json`
7. `find <artifact_dir> -name 'review-report-v*-r*.md'`
8. `ls <artifact_dir>/iterations | wc -l`

`<artifact_dir>` is the review artifact folder under the fixture spec folder, the folder that holds `deep-review-config.json`.

### Expected

Command 1 prints the `--extend` binding and the extension step in both files. Command 2 prints the `run_extended` stem and its payload type. Command 3 exits 0 with no failed test. After command 4 finishes, command 5 prints `1`, command 6 shows the original `sessionId`, generation 1, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3 and `lineageMode` `completed-continue`, command 7 lists one snapshot file, and command 8 shows three more iteration files than before the run.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 and 2 matches, the command 3 summary line, the command 5 count, the command 6 JSON, the command 7 listing and the command 8 counts before and after the run.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: One `run_extended` event, the original `sessionId` and generation, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3, a `completed-continue` lineage, one review report snapshot, no `resumed` row, and exactly three new iteration files.
- **Fail**: Any second `run_extended` event, a changed `sessionId` or generation, a different iteration cap, a missing snapshot, an overwritten snapshot, or an iteration count other than three more.

### Failure Triage

Check the state log first, then the config. A missing event with a correct config means the gateway append was skipped, so read the extension step in `deep-review.yaml` before the classifier override. A correct event with a wrong iteration count points at the classifier, which should land on resume.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Refusal: run the same command with `--extend=0` and confirm it stops with a named usage error before any state write. The flag accepts positive integers only.
- Refusal: run it with `--extend=3` against a review that is still in progress and confirm it refuses because the run is not complete.

---
title: "DR-065 -- Completed-run extension with --extend"
description: "This scenario validates `--extend=N` on a completed deep-research run for `DR-065`. It focuses on same-session continuation, the `run_extended` ledger event and the synthesis snapshot taken before the first new dispatch."
version: 1.0.2.0

# DR-065 -- Completed-run extension with --extend

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-065`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates completed-run extension for `DR-065`. It focuses on `--extend=N` reopening a completed research run in the same session and generation, recording the extension as one `run_extended` event, and copying the prior synthesis to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration starts.

### Why This Matters

An operator who hits the iteration cap on a finished run should be able to buy N more iterations without starting a new session, losing the original session identifier, or overwriting the synthesis the run already produced. If the extension resets the generation, skips the snapshot, or writes a second extension event, the lineage stops being auditable.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a real orchestrator-led check against a completed fixture run rather than a synthetic matrix. The scenario is only complete when the operator can explain, in plain language, how many iterations the extension added and where the prior synthesis went.

- Objective: Verify that `--extend=3` on a completed research run adds exactly three iterations in the same session and generation, records one `run_extended` event, and snapshots the prior synthesis.
- Real user request: The research run finished at its iteration cap. Give it three more iterations without starting a new session.
- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-research run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the synthesis snapshot.`
- Expected execution process: Read the completed fixture config, run the research command with `--extend=3` against that spec folder, then read the state log, the config and the snapshot folder after the run finishes.
- Expected signals: Exactly one `run_extended` event appears, projected as one `extended` row in `research/deep-research-state.jsonl`. The config keeps the original `sessionId` and generation 1, `maxIterations` equals the prior iteration count plus 3, and the lineage mode is `completed-continue`. No `resumed` row is written for the extension. A file matching `synthesis-v*-r*.md` holds the prior synthesis, and exactly three new iteration files are written.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told the run was extended by three iterations, that the same session continues, and where the prior synthesis was kept.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the event count, the session identifier, the iteration cap, the snapshot and the dispatch count all match the expected signals; FAIL if any one of them differs, including a second `run_extended` event or a missing snapshot.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-research run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the synthesis snapshot.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n -- '--extend' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml`
2. `rg -n 'run_extended' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run append-mode-event-legacy-seam.vitest workflow-restart-lifecycle.vitest`
4. `/deep:research "<fixture topic>" :auto --spec-folder=<completed-fixture-spec-folder> --extend=3`
5. `rg -c '"event":"extended"' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-state.jsonl` (the `run_extended` stem lives in the ledger, and the state log carries its projected `extended` row)
6. `jq '{maxIterations, sessionId: .lineage.sessionId, generation: .lineage.generation, lineageMode: .lineage.lineageMode, continuedFromRun: .lineage.continuedFromRun}' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-config.json` (research keeps the lineage fields under `.lineage`)
7. `find <completed-fixture-spec-folder> -name 'synthesis-v*-r*.md'`

### Expected

Command 1 prints the `--extend` binding and the extension step. Command 2 prints the `run_extended` stem and its payload type. Command 3 exits 0 with no failed test. After command 4 finishes, command 5 prints `1`, command 6 shows the original `sessionId`, generation 1, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3 and `lineageMode` `completed-continue`, and command 7 lists one snapshot file. The run adds exactly three iteration files.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 and 2 matches, the command 3 summary line, the command 5 count, the command 6 JSON and the command 7 listing, together with the iteration file count before and after the run.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: One `run_extended` event, the original `sessionId` and generation, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3, a `completed-continue` lineage, one synthesis snapshot, no `resumed` row, and exactly three new iterations.
- **Fail**: Any second `run_extended` event, a changed `sessionId` or generation, a different iteration cap, a missing snapshot, an overwritten snapshot, or a dispatch count other than three.

### Failure Triage

Check the state log first, then the config. A missing event with a correct config means the gateway append was skipped, so read the extension step in `deep-research.yaml` before the classifier override. A correct event with a wrong dispatch count points at the classifier, which should land on resume.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Refusal: run the same command with `--extend=0` and confirm it stops with a named usage error before any state write. The flag accepts positive integers only.
- Refusal: run it against a run that is still in progress and confirm it refuses because the run is not complete.

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the order of the seed step in the workflow and the seed behavior in the admission helper.

- Objective: Confirm that the seed step runs before the fan-out spawn and inside `phase_init` after config creation, and that a second seed does not add a second decomposition.
- Real user request: Start an expansive research run and tell me the frontier already holds open nodes before the first iteration.
- Prompt: `Check that expansive depth mode seeds its depth-one frontier nodes before iteration one, on the fan-out path and on the single-executor path, and that a second seed does not decompose the topic again. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Read the step order in the workflow file, run the ordering cases of the YAML contract suite and run the seed cases of the admission suite by file stem.
- Expected signals: The root `step_frontier_seed` line precedes `step_fanout_spawn`. The `phase_init` copy of `step_frontier_seed` follows `step_create_config`. Two YAML contract cases pass. Two admission cases pass, one showing that a seeded node keeps root as its parent and one showing that a second seed call adds nothing.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say the first iteration starts from open depth-one nodes, and a repeated launch cannot add a second decomposition.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the step order and all four named cases pass; FAIL if the seed runs after the spawn, after the iteration starts, or adds nodes on a repeated call.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that expansive depth mode seeds its depth-one frontier nodes before iteration one, on the fan-out path and on the single-executor path, and that a second seed does not decompose the topic again. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n "^\s*(step_frontier_seed|step_fanout_spawn|step_create_config):" .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision-yaml-contract.vitest -t "seeds" --reporter=verbose`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "seedNodes" --reporter=verbose`

### Expected

Command 1 prints four lines in file order. The first `step_frontier_seed` line comes before the `step_fanout_spawn` line, and the second `step_frontier_seed` line comes after the `step_create_config` line. Command 2 prints `Tests  2 passed | 20 skipped (22)`, with the cases `seeds the frontier before fan-out spawns` and `seeds the single-executor frontier right after the config exists` passing. Command 3 prints `Tests  2 passed | 22 skipped (24)`, with the cases `seedNodes forces root parents` and `seedNodes seeds a run once and skips a second decomposition` passing.

### Evidence

- The four `rg` lines from command 1, read in file order.
- The two verbose case lines and the summary line from commands 2 and 3, with each exit status.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The step order matches the expected order and all four named cases pass.
- **Fail**: The seed step sits after the fan-out spawn or after iteration one starts, or a case fails.

### Failure Triage

- The seed step sits after the spawn: check the root workflow order, because a fan-out lineage skips `phase_init` and would start with no nodes.
- The single-executor seed is missing: check the `phase_init` copy, which must follow `step_create_config`.
- A repeated seed adds nodes: check the already-seeded guard in the admission helper.

# deep-research: Manual Testing Playbook

> **EXECUTION POLICY**: Every scenario MUST be executed for real — not mocked, not stubbed, not classified as "unautomatable". AI agents executing these scenarios must run the actual commands, inspect real files, call real handlers, and verify real outputs. The only acceptable classifications are PASS, FAIL, or SKIP (with a specific sandbox blocker documented). "UNAUTOMATABLE" is not a valid status.

This document combines the manual testing contract for the `deep-research` skill into a single reference. The root playbook acts as the directory, review protocol, and orchestration guide while the per-feature files carry the scenario-specific execution truth.

---

Canonical package artifacts:
- `manual-testing-playbook.md`
- `entry-points-and-modes/`
- `initialization-and-state-setup/`
- `iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/`
- `convergence-and-recovery/`
- `pause-resume-and-fault-tolerance/`
- `synthesis-save-and-guardrails/`
- `command-flow-stress-tests/`
- `fanout/`

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This playbook provides 71 deterministic scenarios across 9 categories validating the current `deep-research` skill surface, including 6 CP-* command-flow stress tests. Each scenario maps to a dedicated feature file with the canonical objective, prompt summary, expected signals, and live source anchors.

### REALISTIC TEST MODEL

1. Start from the operator-visible research workflow, not a synthetic command matrix.
2. Validate public docs and command surfaces before deeper YAML or reducer contracts when that order matters.
3. Capture enough evidence that another operator can reproduce the verdict without re-deriving the scenario.
4. Report a concise user-facing verdict rather than raw implementation notes.

          Accept {review_dimensions} in any of these shapes and convert once:

            - "correctness,security" → ["correctness","security"]

      step_normalize_review_focus:

          fanout-run.cjs applies the same rule in code, so a fan-out lineage receives the same text.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '170,235p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md
### Loop Steps (repeated until convergence)

#### Step 1: Read State
- Read `deep-research-state.jsonl` -- count iterations, get last newInfoRatio
- Read `deep-research-strategy.md` -- get next focus, remaining questions

#### Step 2: Check Convergence
Run the convergence algorithm (see convergence.md):
- Max iterations reached? STOP
- Stuck count >= threshold? STUCK_RECOVERY
- Average newInfoRatio < threshold? STOP candidate: it stops the loop only when the stop policy allows a convergence stop and the quality gates pass
- All questions answered? STOP
- Otherwise: CONTINUE

#### Step 2a: Check Pause Sentinel

Before dispatching, check for a pause sentinel file:

1. Check if `research/.deep-research-pause` exists
2. If present:
   - Log event to JSONL: `{"type":"event","event":"userPaused","mode":"research","run":N,"stopReason":"userPaused","sentinelPath":"{artifact_dir}/.deep-research-pause","timestamp":"<ISO8601>","sessionId":"<sid>","generation":G}`
   - Halt the loop with message:
     ```text
     Research paused. Delete research/.deep-research-pause to resume.
     Current state: Iteration {N}, {remaining} questions remaining.
     ```
   - Do NOT exit to synthesis -- the loop is suspended, not stopped
3. On resume (file deleted and loop restarted):
   - Log event: `{"type":"event","event":"resumed","fromIteration":N}`
   - Continue from step_read_state

Pause and recovery events use the frozen stop-reason enum on the live path. Raw `paused` and `stuck_recovery` labels are legacy aliases and MUST be normalized to `userPaused` and `stuckRecovery` at emission time.

**Use case**: In autonomous mode, this provides the only graceful intervention mechanism short of killing the process. Users can create the sentinel file at any time to pause research between iterations.

#### Step 2b: Generate State Summary

Generate a compact state summary (~200 tokens) for injection into the dispatch prompt:

```text
STATE SUMMARY (auto-generated):
Segment: {current_segment} | Iteration: {N} of {max}
Questions: {answered}/{total} answered | Last focus: {last_focus}
Last 2 ratios: {ratio_N-1} -> {ratio_N} | Stuck count: {stuck_count}
Recovery: {active_recovery_strategy or "none"}
Next focus: {strategy.nextFocus}
```

This summary is prepended to the dispatch context (Step 3) to ensure the agent has baseline context even if detailed strategy.md reading fails or is incomplete. It serves as a redundant context channel.

#### Step 2c: Quality Guard Check

When the convergence algorithm returns STOP:
1. Run quality guard checks (see convergence.md §2.4)
2. Verify minimum coverage, source diversity, and question resolution thresholds
3. If **all guards pass**: proceed with STOP, exit to synthesis
4. If **any guard fails**: override decision to CONTINUE
   - Log each violation: `{"type":"event","event":"guard_violation","guard":"<name>","iteration":N,"detail":"<reason>"}`
   - Persist the blocked legal-stop outcome: `{"type":"event","event":"blocked_stop","mode":"research","run":N,"blockedBy":["<gate>"],"gateResults":{"convergence":{"pass":true,"score":0.0},"keyQuestionCoverage":{"pass":false,"answered":X,"total":Y},"evidenceDensity":{"pass":false,"sources":N},"hotspotSaturation":{"pass":true}},"recoveryStrategy":"<one-line hint>","timestamp":"<ISO8601>","sessionId":"<sid>","generation":G}`
   - Append failed guard details to strategy.md "Active Risks" section
5. The loop continues until BOTH convergence AND quality guards pass simultaneously
6. Guard checks apply only to STOP decisions - CONTINUE and STUCK_RECOVERY bypass this step

If the legal-stop decision tree returns `blocked`, the workflow MUST append the `blocked_stop` JSONL event before continuing. Reducers and dashboards consume the persisted event; they must not infer blocked-stop state solely from prose logs.
[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && rg -n "minIterations|frontierOpen|min iterations|frontier" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md | head -30
411:Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of follow-up questions beside the key questions seeded at init. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 per depth, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to `maxIterations`. A run without the flag keeps the default path and renders the same bytes as before.
413:`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the topic into 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. It calls `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`.
415:A leaf may append `frontier_proposal` rows to its delta only when its prompt carries a FRONTIER block, which `frontier-admit.cjs block` computes from the node's remaining child slots and the prompt pack renders through `{frontier_block}`. `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and calls `frontier-admit.cjs admit`, which applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. The first failure prunes the proposal with that reason. Admitted nodes become `research/inbox.jsonl` rows with `origin: "frontier"`.
419:Under stuck recovery the handler closes the focused node as `exhausted` with reason `stuck` through `frontier-admit.cjs close`, keeps the `stuckRecovery` event, and moves to the next open node. Global recovery runs only when no node is open.
421:On a fan-out run, `fanout-run.cjs --depth-mode expansive` deals the seeded open depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order across lineages, writes each lineage's `frontier-nodes.jsonl` and inbox rows before it spawns, binds `frontier_scope:` in the lineage prompt and records `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. `fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers, each node keeping its lineage label, and records a partition guard that names any dealt node no lineage reports.

# DRV-068 -- Cross-mode anti-convergence contract

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-068`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `deep-review` slice of the cross-mode anti-convergence contract. The objective is to confirm that review mode declares a minimum iteration floor, exposes a fail-closed runtime stop policy, resolves capabilities through the shared runtime helper, and protects the contract from optimizer drift.

### WHY THIS MATTERS

Review loops can produce convincing but incomplete early STOP decisions when only one pass has run. The anti-convergence floor makes that early stop illegal unless the configured minimum has been satisfied, while the runtime and optimizer guards keep capability loading and tuning from weakening the stop policy.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators should run this as a deterministic documentation-and-config check against the shipped files.

- Objective: Verify `deep-review` has `antiConvergence.minIterations: 2`, `convergenceMode: "default"`, `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`, a runtime capability matrix with fail-closed stop policy, and optimizer invariant locks for convergence mode plus `minIterations <= maxIterations`.
- Real user request: Check whether deep-review can stop after one iteration or tune around its anti-convergence floor.
- Prompt: `Validate the deep-review anti-convergence floor, fail-closed stop policy, runtime capability resolver, and optimizer invariant guard.`
- Expected execution process: Inspect the review config and runtime capability matrix, then inspect the shared runtime resolver and optimizer manifest.
- Desired user-facing outcome: The user understands that review mode declares a 2-iteration floor, fails closed when runtime stop policy is missing or wrong, and rejects optimizer candidates where the floor exceeds `maxIterations`.
- Expected signals: `antiConvergence.minIterations` equals `2`; `stopPolicy` equals `fail-closed`; `runtime-capabilities.cjs` rejects missing or non-fail-closed stop policy; optimizer manifest locks convergence mode and carries `minIterations<=maxIterations`.
- Pass/fail posture: PASS if all four anchors agree. FAIL if any anchor is missing, permissive, or contradictory.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### RECOMMENDED ORCHESTRATION PROCESS

1. Restate the user request in plain language.
2. Follow the command sequence in order.
3. Capture grep outputs for every expected signal.
4. Return a concise verdict with any drift called out explicitly.

### Prompt

Validate the deep-review anti-convergence floor, fail-closed stop policy, runtime capability resolver, and optimizer invariant guard.

### Commands

1. `bash: rg -n '"antiConvergence"|"minIterations": 2|"convergenceMode": "default"|"stopPolicy": "fail-closed"' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json`
2. `bash: rg -n '"stopPolicy": "fail-closed"' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/runtime-capabilities.json`
3. `bash: rg -n 'missing stopPolicy|stopPolicy must be "fail-closed"|validateStopPolicy' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/runtime-capabilities.cjs`
4. `bash: rg -n 'antiConvergence.convergenceMode|convergenceMode|minIterations<=maxIterations|minIterations|maxIterations' .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/optimizer/optimizer-manifest.json`

### Expected

The config declares the review floor and fail-closed policy, the runtime matrix repeats fail-closed policy, the shared resolver rejects missing or invalid stop policy, and the optimizer manifest locks convergence mode while requiring `minIterations <= maxIterations`.

### Evidence

Capture the matching lines from the four command outputs.

### Pass/Fail

PASS if all commands return the expected anchors with no contradictory values. FAIL if `minIterations`, `stopPolicy`, resolver validation, or optimizer invariant metadata is absent or permissive.

### Failure Triage

Privilege the live config and runtime helper over older references. If the optimizer manifest path changes, use `rg --files .skilled/skills | rg 'optimizer-manifest\.json$'` and rerun the invariant check against the discovered path.

   121	## 3. LIVE ALGORITHM
   122	
   123	### Decision Order
   124	
   125	```text
   126	1. If iteration count >= maxIterations, STOP with maxIterationsReached.
   127	2. If all key questions have evidence-backed answers, nominate STOP.
   128	3. If stuckCount >= stuckThreshold, enter stuck recovery.
   129	4. Compute composite convergence signals.
   130	5. If weighted stop score > 0.60, nominate STOP.
   131	6. Evaluate legal-stop gates for every STOP candidate except hard max-iteration stop.
   132	7. Block every non-terminal STOP until a graph decision exists.
   133	8. If gates pass, pivot for an eligible divergent reason or STOP normally; otherwise emit blockedStop and CONTINUE with recovery focus.
   134	```
   135	
   136	### Composite Signal Weights
   137	
   138	| Signal | Weight | Min Iterations | Measures |
   139	|--------|--------|----------------|----------|
   140	| Rolling Average | `0.30` | 3 evidence iterations | Recent information yield |
   141	| MAD Noise Floor | `0.35` | 4 evidence iterations | Signal vs noise in `newInfoRatio` |
   142	| Question Entropy | `0.35` | 1 key question | Evidence-backed question coverage |
   143	
   144	The weighted stop score is normalized over active signals. STOP is nominated only when the normalized score is greater than `0.60`. See `convergence-signals.md` for exact signal rules and reporting.
   145	
   146	---
   147	
   148	## 4. LEGAL STOP GATES
   149	
   150	Every non-terminal STOP candidate must pass the gate bundle before the workflow exits.

    21	---
    22	
    23	## 2. HOW IT WORKS
    24	
    25	The live loop applies convergence in layers. Hard stops for `maxIterations` and all-questions-answered run first. convergence.cjs evaluates the weighted `shouldContinue()` signals from `convergence.md`, and when those signals or full question coverage nominate STOP, it applies the legal-stop bundle rather than stopping on novelty math alone.
    26	
    27	That legal-stop bundle is workflow-visible, not only prose guidance. The workflow runs convergence.cjs with `--persist-snapshot` and `--stop-decision`, records the graph convergence result, and persists a first-class `blocked_stop` event when the decision is blocked. That event carries gate results, blockers, recovery guidance, session identifier, and generation so the reducer and dashboard can show why the loop had to continue.

---

### Completed-run extension (--extend)

#### Description

A completed deep-review run extends in place by N iterations under --extend=N, keeps its session identifier and generation, and keeps the prior review report as a snapshot before re-synthesis.

#### How It Works

The run continues under lineage mode `completed-continue`, keeps the prior review report as a snapshot before re-synthesis and takes `--extend-minutes=M` on the duration budget. Extension is refused with fan-out.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md`](loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Review minIterations floor

#### Description

A review run with a configured minIterations floor returns CONTINUE with minIterationsNotReached until its iteration count reaches the floor, so a review never stops before the floor.

#### How It Works

`convergence.cjs` returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached` below the floor, and the command's fixed stop order checks the floor before the gates. The shipped default is 2.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/min-iterations-floor.md`](loop-lifecycle/min-iterations-floor.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)

#### Description

Opt-in --depth-mode=expansive on deep-review seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target, keeps node status in review/frontier-nodes.jsonl, and holds a review stop while nodes stay open.

#### How It Works

Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, open nodes count as `searchDebt`, and the divergent pivot waits for a terminal frontier. The live pilot runs are still open, so no pilot result is recorded here.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md`](loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

## 3. STATE MANAGEMENT

These entries describe the review packet files, how they change over time, and how reducer-owned state feeds the loop, dashboard, and synthesis surfaces.

### JSONL state log

#### Description

Stores the append-only execution history for the review lineage.

#### How It Works

`deep-review-state.jsonl` begins with a config record, appends iteration records with severity-weighted findings and coverage data, records pause and blocked-stop and synthesis events, and can carry graph convergence and graph event payloads for review-aware coverage tracking.

#### Source Files

See [`state-management/jsonl-state-log.md`](../feature-catalog/state-management/jsonl-state-log.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Strategy tracking

#### Description

Maintains the mutable review brain across fresh agent dispatches.

#### How It Works

`deep-review-strategy.md` tracks remaining and completed dimensions, running findings, worked and failed approaches, cross-reference status, files under review, and the next focus anchor that steers the next iteration.

#### Source Files

See [`state-management/strategy-tracking.md`](../feature-catalog/state-management/strategy-tracking.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Config management

#### Description

Defines the immutable packet contract for the current review lineage.

#### How It Works

`deep-review-config.json` captures the topic, target type, configured dimensions, lineage fields, thresholds, protection levels, and reducer contract. It is written once during init and becomes the stable reference for the rest of the run.

#### Source Files

See [`state-management/config-management.md`](../feature-catalog/state-management/config-management.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && nl -ba .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md | sed -n '24,36p'
24	The expansive run seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1. Leaf `frontier_proposal` rows are admitted under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps. Merge commits are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff`, and index rebuilds are pruned as `generated-artifact`.
    25	
    26	### Dimension Coverage
    27	
    28	The active node's next uncovered dimension is dispatched. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so `--max-iterations` should be at least `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` for the depth reached.
    29	
    30	### Stop Behavior
    31	
    32	A review stop candidate becomes another iteration while nodes stay open. The `frontierOpen` veto applies to the review stop candidates, including `all_dimensions_clean` and the composite stop, after the minIterations floor.
    33	
    34	### Pivot
    35	
    36	The divergent review pivot runs only once the frontier is terminal. It seeds from terminal unanswered nodes and routes accepted candidates through admission.

function graphHotspotSaturation(graph) {
  if (!isPlainObject(graph) || graph.failed === true) return null;
  const trace = Array.isArray(graph.trace) ? graph.trace : [];
  const entry = trace.find(
    (candidate) => isPlainObject(candidate) && candidate.signal === 'hotspotSaturation',
  );
  return entry !== undefined && typeof entry.passed === 'boolean' ? entry.passed : null;
}

- If STUCK_RECOVERY: modify focus directive, reset stuck count, continue
- Otherwise: increment iteration counter, go to Step 1

### Expansive Depth Frontier (opt-in)

Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of follow-up questions beside the key questions seeded at init. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 per depth, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to `maxIterations`. A run without the flag keeps the default path and renders the same bytes as before.

`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the topic into 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. It calls `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`.

A leaf may append `frontier_proposal` rows to its delta only when its prompt carries a FRONTIER block, which `frontier-admit.cjs block` computes from the node's remaining child slots and the prompt pack renders through `{frontier_block}`. `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and calls `frontier-admit.cjs admit`, which applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. The first failure prunes the proposal with that reason. Admitted nodes become `research/inbox.jsonl` rows with `origin: "frontier"`.

Next Focus picks the shallowest open node first, then the least visited, then the oldest, and writes `Frontier node <id> (depth N, scope S): <question>` so a visit does not read as an answer.

Under stuck recovery the handler closes the focused node as `exhausted` with reason `stuck` through `frontier-admit.cjs close`, keeps the `stuckRecovery` event, and moves to the next open node. Global recovery runs only when no node is open.

On a fan-out run, `fanout-run.cjs --depth-mode expansive` deals the seeded open depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order across lineages, writes each lineage's `frontier-nodes.jsonl` and inbox rows before it spawns, binds `frontier_scope:` in the lineage prompt and records `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. `fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers, each node keeping its lineage label, and records a partition guard that names any dealt node no lineage reports.

### Ideas Backlog Convention

The canonical ideas backlog is reducer-owned and derived from JSONL lifecycle events. A persistent ideas file at `research/research-ideas.md` may still exist as operator-authored context, but leaf agents record new machine-discovered tangents with `idea_observed` events.

   102	
   103	#### Expansive Depth Mode
   104	
   105	`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which is set to `maxIterations` when the config is created. Both the mode and the block are locked against the optimizer. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
   106	
   107	After the review floor, an expansive run that still holds an open or active node turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`, whichever candidate it was, `all_dimensions_clean` or a composite convergence vote. The veto never creates a stop of its own and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the `minIterations` floor. Under stuck recovery the handler closes the active node as `exhausted` with reason `stuck`, so a node the run cannot fund stops holding the veto open.
   108	
   109	#### Divergent Pivot Contract
   110	
   111	Review has one eligible reason: the exact internal reason `all_dimensions_clean` with decision `STOP`. The nine legal-stop gates run before the divergent branch and remain unchanged. `maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `stuckRecovery`, `error`, `manualStop`, `userPaused` and `maxDurationReached` are explicitly excluded. Max iterations, pause or cancellation, manual stop, unrecoverable error, a reached duration limit and any blocked security or quality gate therefore never dispatch a pivot Council.
   112	
   160	
   161	**Key defaults:** `maxIterations=7`, `convergenceThreshold=0.10`, `rollingStopThreshold=0.08`, `noProgressThreshold=0.05`, `stuckThreshold=2`, `minStabilizationPasses=1`
   162	
   163	**P0 override:** Any new P0 finding sets `newFindingsRatio >= 0.50`, blocking convergence.
   164	
   165	**Frontier veto:** Under `--depth-mode=expansive` an open or active frontier node turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor.

A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.

 ### Decision Priority
 
-Checks are evaluated in this order (first match wins):
-
-1. **Max iterations** -- hard cap, always checked first.
-2. **All dimensions covered + clean** -- every configured dimension covered (the four by default), no active P0/P1, stabilization passed, gates passed.
-3. **Stuck detection** -- 2+ consecutive no-progress iterations.
-4. **Composite convergence** -- 3-signal weighted vote with threshold 0.60.
-5. **Legal-stop gate bundle** -- review-specific stop gates. If a stop vote fails any gate, persist `stopReason=blockedStop` and continue.
-6. **Default CONTINUE** -- none of the above triggered.
+Checks are evaluated in this order (first match wins), as `buildStopDecision` in `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` runs them:
+
+1. **Max iterations** -- hard cap, always checked first. It outranks every veto below.
+2. **Consecutive failures** -- three trailing failed iterations stop the run with stop reason `error`.
+3. **Stuck detection** -- `stuckThreshold` (2) consecutive no-progress iterations route to stuck recovery.
+4. **Convergence mode off** -- `convergenceMode: off` returns `CONTINUE` (`convergenceModeOff`).
+5. **Stop policy max-iterations** -- `stopPolicy: max-iterations` returns `CONTINUE` (`stopPolicyMaxIterations`).
+6. **Stop candidate** -- all dimensions covered and clean (every configured dimension covered, the four by default, no active P0/P1, coverage stabilized) is a hard candidate; otherwise the 3-signal composite vote with threshold 0.60 is a soft one. No candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`noStopCandidate`).
+7. **Iteration floor** -- below `minIterations` the candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`minIterationsNotReached`).
+8. **Frontier veto** -- under `--depth-mode=expansive`, an open frontier node turns the candidate into `CONTINUE` (`frontierOpen`).
+9. **Legal-stop gate bundle** -- review-specific stop gates. If the candidate fails any gate, persist `stopReason=blockedStop` and continue.
+10. **Graph decision** -- the candidate is combined with the graph convergence verdict: `STOP_ALLOWED` stops, `STOP_BLOCKED` blocks the stop and `CONTINUE` continues the run.

          parentSessionId: "{parent_session_id_init}"
          lineageMode: "{lineage_mode_init}"
          generation: "{generation_init}"
          continuedFromRun: null
          reviewTarget: "{review_target}"
          reviewTargetType: "{review_target_type}"
          # Reuse the single normalized init-time value from step_normalize_review_dimensions.

          # Null when review_focus_normalized is empty. The review report reads it back to show the focus.
          reviewFocus: "{review_focus_normalized}"
          resource_map_present: "{resource_map_present}"
          reviewScopeFiles: "{review_scope_files}"
          severityThreshold: "P2"
                  priorMaxDurationMinutes,
                  newMaxDurationMinutes,
                  elapsedMinutesAtExtension,
                },
              },
            };
            const next = {
              ...config,
              status: "initialized",
              maxIterations: newMaxIterations,
              maxDurationMinutes: newMaxDurationMinutes,
              lineageMode: "completed-continue",
              continuedFromRun,
            };
            fs.writeFileSync(path.join(workDir, "event.json"), JSON.stringify(event));
            fs.writeFileSync(path.join(workDir, "config.json"), JSON.stringify(next, null, 2) + "\n");
            const stopPolicy = config.antiConvergence && config.antiConvergence.stopPolicy || "convergence";
            // Review has no question inbox, so under convergence an unchanged dimension set
            // can re-stop on all_dimensions_clean before the new budget is used.
            const warning = stopPolicy === "convergence"
              ? "This run stops on convergence, so it may re-stop on all_dimensions_clean right away. Pass --convergence-mode=off or use stopPolicy max-iterations to keep it running."
